## Grand Prix Yokohama 2012

[日本語の記事へ](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808205200/https://magic.wizards.com/en/node/493956) 

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/6d/ce/6dce4ea5da6cd86617171aeece8be36c7a6f57292d1b937ddcf5f522faa7e2c9.jpg)

Jyun'ichi Miyajima is the Grand Prix Yokohama Champion!

One of the few players piloting the White-Black Tokens deck in the room this weekend, Miyajima deftly disrupted, attacked and destroyed his way through a crowded field to be the last man standing. And who would have predicted before the weekend started that the finals would pit White-Black Tokens against Mono Blue Faeries for the trophy?

But the weekend will also be remembered for the breakout performance of Naya Pod, which put four local players in the Top 8 despite a dearth of byes between them. It will be remembered as [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel)'s coming out party in Modern and for a Top 8 that was showed that rogue could be the way to go if you knew your deck well.

Congratulations to Jyun'ichi Miyajima and all of this weekend's Top 8 players!

## Quarterfinals - Toshiyuki Kadooka vs. Yuki Yotsumoto

*by Blake Rasmussen* 

A very cool story has emerged as the Grand Prix Yokohama Top 8 has taken shape. A local play group, most with fewer than three byes coming into the weekend, might just have broken the format.

Youichi Nagami, Yuki Yotsumoto, Masaki Ushijima and Toshiyuki Kadooka are all playing similar Naya Pod decks built to utilize the interaction between [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) and, well, everything. Between the four of them they had exactly four byes, the bulk of which came from Kadooka's three free passes. Only Nagami had even a single bye.

With so many Naya Pod decks in the Top 8, one mirror match was practically inevitable, and those odds fell on Toshiyuki Kadooka and Yuki Yotsumoto.

Kadooka is the most accomplished of the three, having lost in the finals of Pro Tour Nagoya 2011 to David Sharfman.

But that doesn't mean we should count out Yotsumoto just yet. He had a much harder road to get to the Top 8, starting with no byes and winding his way to the Top 8.

### Game 1

The mirror started off as mirrors of this sort do, with both players playing multiple mana creatures. Yotsumoto played a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), passed the turn and then...conceded?

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ec/be/ecbe14f86ff1b5540ec92e4bfd24b2674e528e20f83b12fbc90f8b41fbbcf95e.jpg)

*Yuki Yotsumoto was quick in Game 1 ... Quick to concede to [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence)*

Apparently he just can't beat the [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) and admitted as much as soon as Kadooka cast it.

Still too early to count out Yotsumoto...assuming he had an answer or 10 for Linvala somewhere in his sideboard.

Kadooka 1 – Yotsumoto 0

### Game 2

Kadooka came out of the games again, casting a turn three Linvala, only this time Yotsumoto had access to [Combust](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Combust) and quickly dispatched the offending legend (one answer). When Kadooka played a second, Totsumoto played his own copy to bury them both (two answers).

Stymied for the time being, Kadooka used a [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) to off a [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage). Yotsumoto had spent a number of his resources making sure he didn't die to Linvala, and only managed a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise).

That, too, was short for the world, as Kiki-Jiki copied [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) to kill it.

However, that let Yotsumoto [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness), returning Linvala, which now threatened to, finally, effectively keep the silence.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4f/f3/4ff30eaff80de063af626a2a4b2b3abb52c3a162b83e5453570f55a63ed70be7.jpg)

*Toshiyuki Kadooka has come within one match of winning a Pro Tour. Will he pick up a trophy this weekend?* 

Shut down from actually using creature abilities, Kadooka took a new route. With two Exalted creatures, [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) and [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) could start getting in one at a time.

However, Kadooka found a better plan waiting at the top of his deck. [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) returned Linvala, allowing him to unlock his Kiki-Jiki.

Facing board he couldn't match, Yotsumoto conceded once his Linvala, the only thing holding back Kadooka's hordes, was gone.

Kadooka 2 – Yotsumoto 0

## Quarterfinals - Youichi Nagami vs. Jyun’ichi Miyajima

*by Blake Rasmussen* 

This wasn't the first time these two players had met this weekend. Way back in Round 14 Jyun'ichi Miyajima took down two games on his way to a Top 8 berth. Turns out it didn't hurt Youichi Nagami too much, as he found a way to get to the quarterfinals as well.

Their decks were a study in contrast. Nagami's Naya Pod deck was probably the deck of the tournament, but used large creatures, tutors and combos for its end game. Miyajima, on the other hand, had to grind out wins with a bevy of tokens and discard spells to keep his advantage.

### Game 1

Miyajima took five damage right off the bat to fetch a [Godless Shrine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Godless+Shrine) and [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), revealing [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod), [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker) and lands, binning the deck's namesake [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod).

Nagami played the [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) the following turn and followed up with a [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer).

Meanwhile, Miyajima took a page out of pre-banning *Innistrad* Block Constructed with [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue) followed by [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), plus a flashback the following turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a2/bd/a2bd93d94a63d7dbf852ab6a46684b3ec1c0fe44475f2212f3bd442a05749db3.jpg)

*Youichi Nagami had battled his way from zero byes to the Top 8. Could he keep it going in the quarterfinals?* 

But in the end it wasn't enough. Kiki-Jiki came into play and made a copy of [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer), keeping [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) safely tucked on the top of Nagami's library from the [Tidehollow Sculler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidehollow+Sculler) that followed.

Nagami simply used the powerful green tutor to search up [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) and, combined with Kiki Jiki, he was able to attack with as many 3/4 flying Angels as he wanted.

Nagami 1 – Miyajima 0

### Game 2

Another turn one [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) revealed [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff), [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) and [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast), discarding the Pontiff.

Nagami played the Seer and Melira over the next two turns, but unfortunately lost his [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) to a [Tidehollow Sculler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidehollow+Sculler). Miyajima soon [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember)ed Melira and started to lay down some pressure with [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue) and [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession). A few turns of that and a few blank draws from Nagami and we were even at one game apiece.

Nagami 1 – Miyajima 1

### Game 3

Unlike their Round 14 feature match, this one had found its way to a Game 3. And what a Game 3 it was. Winner keeps playing with a shot at the trophy, all while earning an invite to the Pro Tour, while the other player got just a memory (well, and a fair bit of money).

Nagami led with [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) as Miyajima skipped his typical first turn [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to play a [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) followed by a [Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution), whipping away Nagami's start.

[Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) let Nagami reload with the formerly dead [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), but Miyajima had his own deadly three drop with [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls).

[Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) were next for Nagami, building his mana base but short on action. He was already down to two cards in hand.

Attacking with the two Spirit tokens and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) let Miyajima trigger his [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights), flipping out a key [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage) post combat. The Melira combo wasn't viable as long as the artifact remained in play.

Things got even worse for Nagami the next turn as [Honor of the Pure](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Honor+of+the+Pure) made the Spirit tokens larger, and when [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) attempted to jump in front of a token, the Black-White player had the [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember). The tokens started methodically clocking the Naya Pod player for four in the air.

Still, the [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) had allowed Nagami to blink his [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness), returning [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer). He cast it the following turn while also activating [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) to pump his team.

The next turn, he finally got to attacking, moving in with a 3/2 [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) and 2/2 [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer).

Miyajima kept hitting back, though, using [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) to clear a, well, path, for his two Spirit Tokens by exiling [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise). Four more damage put Nagami in lethal range of the rampaging spirits.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e2/6c/e26c32f88e791204abb9229307d259f2d55686ca99782dd6a0053896931a405f.jpg)

*Jyun'ichi Miyajima's Spirit tokens methodically hit for four damage, turn after turn after turn after...* 

[Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) revealed a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) at the top of Nagami's deck, and suddenly it felt as if he might be able to survive the next attack.

Miyajima was forced to block the Witness with [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to stay alive, but was itching to attack back with his lethal spirits. He untapped, immediately moved to attack and watched as Nagami flashed in the [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) that would save his life...

...except for the [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) Miyajima had to dispatch the potential guardian angel. Sweeping aside the weekend's premier winged warrior, the two Spirits delivered the final points of damage.

Nagami 1 – Miyajima 2

## Quarterfinals - Kei Umehara vs. Hiroya Miyamoto

*by Chapman Sim* 

Kei Umehara's deck is rather special. Armed with only [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island)s, [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and [Faerie Conclave](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faerie+Conclave)s, he was able to navigate past the last sixteen rounds without the need of an additional color. Admittedly, he does play with [Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles), which is capable of world domination when backed by countermagic and [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite)s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ef/8d/ef8d53a3d31b475730ca1178d7775465ad934f1a3dbf397e6b7406d423443763.jpg)

*Kei Umehara and Hiroya Miyamoto shake hands before the match*

His opponent wasn't going to let him be. Aside from the usual robots (we're referring to [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager)), Hiroya Miyamoto also recruited the help of [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium), one of the biggest monsters that also pumps his entire army.

Both players were aware how important this quarterfinal game would be. The winner would earn the right to participate in the upcoming Pro Tour in Seattle, while the other would have to be content with "just" a Top 8 appearance.

### Game One

Miyamoto was ranked higher in Swiss and had the privilege of starting first.

He opened with merely [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal) and [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), not quite as fast a hand as he desired. All his opponent did was to lay an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and pass the turn, keeping up mana for the [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare) which nabbed Miyamoto's [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager).

![Master of Etherium](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Master+of+Etherium)

[Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) met with [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) but it resolved on the next turn anyway. When Miyamoto tried to add a gigantic droid onto the board ([Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium)), [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) was at the ready. He simply attacked with [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite) and put +1/+1 counters on both his creatures.

[Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles) came to the rescue for Umehara but he had to think twice when Miyamoto tried to resolve [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager). He eventually nodded in approval and tried to snatch [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) as soon as he could, but Miyamoto simply sacrificed it in response.

On his own turn, Umehara wasted no time gaining control of [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and it seemed like he was in firm control of the game. The pressure from Miyamoto was gentle, only two damage a turn from [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) and [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus).

Umehara grew [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) up to a 6/6 and added [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique), [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) and [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) to the board. Unable to mount a profitable attack, Miyamoto passed the turn, hoping to draw something to change the state of the game.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/22/6e/226e815c758079f829b8d1a25a35360489fdef9662062b277194e9076f087eaf.jpg)

*Kei Umehara takes the first game*

His prayers were answered and his deck served up [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating), which convinced [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) to stay home. Both players had seven creatures a piece (including Umehara's [Faerie Conclave](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faerie+Conclave) & [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s, and Miyamoto's Blinkmoth Nexi). Now at just six paltry life, Umehara was forced to match the creature count each turn, for an instant-speed [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) activation would make any unblocked creature lethal. The stalemate was broken when Umehara topdecked another [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), tapping down the opposite side for the win.

Kei Umehara 1 – Hiroya Miyamoto 0

### Game Two

Miyamoto's threats continued to be systematically handled by Umehara's countermagic-heavy deck. [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare) repelled [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) met with [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), leaving Miyamoto with only [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) and [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter).

When Umehara called forth [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) to block, his opponent made use of this window to cast [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium), the very last card in his hand. At this point, the Vedalken Wizard was contributing ten power to the board (it being 7/7 and granting +1/+1 to three other critters) and giving Umehara a massive brain aneurysm.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f7/fa/f7faadb1b23345c632864aead1df0e791bd7ba5b8cd4ca1b675a3062331eb903.jpg)

*Hiroya Miyamoto* 

After making sure Miyamoto had drawn nothing dangerous, he the freshly summoned [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) to trade with [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge), taking a huge hit going down to six life and then

A second [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) countered [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating), returning [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium) to his hand. Refusing to run into [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), Miyamoto deliberated it was better to resummon it the next turn. When he did, it was countered with [Disrupting Shoal](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Shoal) but that left him weak to [Etched Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Oracle) and [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast) which finished him off the very next turn.

Kei Umehara 1 – Hiroya Miyamoto 1

### Game Three

Miyamoto developed his mana with [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal) and [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), but his first creature ([Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter)) was turned to dust by [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite).

[Pendelhaven](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pendelhaven) entered play next turn and Umehara wasted no time sending in the little faerie for two damage, leaving just enough mana to use [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) on [Etched Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Oracle). [Engineered Explosives](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Explosives) for zero reduced Miyamoto's board to just [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum) and [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), while he continued to attack with [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and a 2/3 [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite).

![Engineered Explosives](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Engineered+Explosives)

![Pendelhaven](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Pendelhaven)

Taking advantage of his opponent's creature-light hand, a second [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) hit play to flick away [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite). When Miyamoto drew another [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), Umehara flashed in a third copy of the faerie. The audience winced in pain, as though being seared by a hot pan.

Umehara animated both his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s and went all-in with his triple [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite). As a last-ditch effort, Miyamoto tried to use [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast) to stay alive. Umehara revealed the final two cards in his hand ([Disrupting Shoal](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Shoal) and [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare)), ending the match the way [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) would also have.

Kei Umehara 2 – Hiroya Miyamoto 1

## Quarterfinals - Satoshi Yamaguchi vs. Masaki Ushijima

*by Chapman Sim* 

Surviving sixteen rounds is no easy task, and even more so if you weren't able to secure any byes. It is interesting to note that Masaki Ushijima had made it thus far with no byes at all, a rather amazing accomplishment, seeing how he had actually won more played matches than some of his fellow Top 8 competitors.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3d/d3/3dd3bcab4605bbb2a8e9611824a44ed668ccc822d4b7291742c5075da21e0926.jpg)

*Satoshi Yamaguchi and Masaki Ushijima prepare to battle*

Piloting one of the four Naya Pod decks to break into the Top 8 today, there was no longer any doubt of the archetype's power. In this matchup, he would face Satoshi Yamaguchi, who was the only the only Jund player left in the entire tournament. Despite strong numbers, Jund had only managed to put one player in the Top 8, but Yamaguchi was prepared to dispel the notion that his deck's heyday was over.

### Game One

Ushijima was forced to take a mulligan but he kept his next six, and they were off. Yamaguchi led with [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek), (causing his opponent to reveal [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling)) and eventually decided to make him ditch [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/38/93/389308c6602915b5351f39da5fccf781c5bbf228a502194de135b8766ad931d2.jpg)

*Lands are hard to find for Masaki Ushijima in Game One*

[Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) made an entrance on turn two, gifting Yamaguchi [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) the next turn. [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) joined the board, while Ushijima could only stare in bewilderment as he was stuck on just two lands, [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township). [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil) joined the fray, and Ushijima decided it was time to move on.

Satoshi Yamaguchi 1 – Masaki Ushijima 0

### Game Two

Yamaguchi was quick to point [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) at the opposing [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), and used [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to expel [Phantasmal Image](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phantasmal+Image) from his opponent's hand, allowing him to keep [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod).

The reason was apparent, when Yamaguchi dropped [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage) and [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) onto the board soon after. [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) tried to stave off the 3/2 but when Yamaguchi added another 3/2 to the board ([Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf)), a cascaded [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate) ensured that both would attack unimpeded.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b0/ec/b0ecf0b087323656e09fc85b023703224cefc30644b2fbb256bb25cb7f85979b.jpg)

*Satoshi Yamaguchi deck advances him to the Semifinals*

With [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage) effectively neutralizing his entire strategy, it seemed that the game would be over soon enough, but Ushijima did not give up just yet. He summoned [Reveillark](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reveillark), and stared at the incoming 9 damage on the board (if you add in an animated [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village)), praying for the best.

Yamaguchi sent in all three attackers, proceeded to place two +1/+1 counters [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village), dealing exactly 8 damage to seal the match and advance to the semifinals.

Satoshi Yamaguchi 2 – Masaki Ushijima 0

## Semifinals - Kei Umehara vs. Satoshi Yamaguchi

*by Chapman Sim* 

### Game One

Yamaguchi was first to play and he was able to successfully resolve [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil), putting his opponent under immediate pressure on both his life totals and his precious grip of cards.

Umehara wasted no time summoning [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) and then [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) to finally rid the opposing Planeswalker. He did take 6 damage from the incoming [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village), going down to 13.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/85/83/8583e3193b2340f2a6a43a79fbecbfeeb55c45a029a51a4a80847381d85bc072.jpg)

*Kei and Satoshi are off to an intense first game*

Thereafter, Yamagushi attempted to kill the 3/1 flier, by mistakenly pointing [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) at it, to the amusement of the crowd. He rectified the blunder and put [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) into his graveyard instead. When he tried [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) next turn, he gained [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) from the cascade, but Umehara decided to counter it, bouncing [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) in the process.

[Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) crashed into the red zone again, and [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) jumped out of Umehara's hand, trading with the manland and going down to five life. Finding no solution to the unfriendly army, Umehara scooped up his cards.

Kei Umehara 0 – Satoshi Yamaguchi 1

### Game Two

The game proceeded slowly, almost crawling to a drone. Not that Umehara was complaining, he was glad to not be under any pressure.

The real action only began when [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) entered play on turn six. It was then that Yamaguchi decided to recruit [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), cascading into a timely [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge). Umehara summoned a second [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite), and kept it alive with [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare) when his opponent tried to shatter it.

[Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) finally shed some light on why the game had progressed so slowly. Aside from two [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate) and two [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Bloodbraid%2BElf), Yamaguchi had drawn ten lands and nothing else. When Yamaguchi finally drew a threat, it was conveniently two-costed and was the ideal candidate to be stolen with [Threads of Disloyalty](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Threads+of+Disloyalty).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d8/6a/d86aaec2ef7f9f1c0438fe5346add38ee277c5412349926814311a33e3055c11.jpg)

*Despite having taken the first game, his Yamagushi's deck was unkind to him in the second round*

[Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) robbed Yamaguchi of another turn, as the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) which had betrayed its master took another huge chunk of its owner's life. Umehara revealed [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from his hand, explaining amiably that there was no way he could lose this one. Yamaguchi agreed and they were off to the rubber game.

Kei Umehara 1 – Satoshi Yamaguchi 1

### Game Three

[Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) from Yamaguchi forced Umehara to discard [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), leaving his opponent with just [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) and five land. It seemed like Umehara had made a errant judgment (as anyone watching the match would have to agree), keeping a hand with no action at all.

How wrong could we possibly get?

The [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) popped out on turn two, and was methodically joined by [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) on turn three, followed by [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) on turn four, and then [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) on turn five! Umehara's deck had bestowed upon him the nut draw!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f1/07/f10794fd74d9b12058403ee29bd622a0ccac9e30cd74656378762f69ed29dd8f.jpg)

*Congratulations to Kei Umehara who advances to the Finals!*

Having not drawn any sources of green mana until now, Yamaguchi could only extend his hand and congratulate his opponent graciously. He then quietly snuck his grip of [Seal of Primordium](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Primordium), [Thrun, the Last Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrun%2C+the+Last+Troll) and [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge) back into his deck box, mildly bothered to be denied of his third color at such a crucial juncture of the tournament.

It is written. Kei Umehara was destined to win this match. He proceeds to the finals.

Kei Umehara 2 – Satoshi Yamaguchi 1

## Semifinals - Jyun’ichi Miyajima vs. Toshiyuki Kadooka

*by Blake Rasmussen* 

If you had asked any number of players what decks would be battling for the right to hoist the trophy this weekend, it seems pretty unlikely three of the four decks now sitting in the quarterfinals would be on anyone's list. Over on one side of the feature match area we have Mono Blue Faeries facing down Jund.

On this side of the bracket we have two rogue-ish strategies. Toshiyuki Kadooka's Naya Pod is probably the deck of the tournament, putting four players in the Top 8, most of them with few byes to pad their stats. Miyajima's WB tokens isn't a terribly explosive or over the top powerful deck, but it plays a strong disruptive game with [Tidehollow Sculler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidehollow+Sculler) and [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) plus removal, all backed by an army of token creatures.

### Game 1

Leading with a [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp), Miyajima used a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to reveal [Village Bell-Ringer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Village+Bell-Ringer), [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite), [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) and lands, with the [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) hitting the bin.

[Raise the Alarm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raise+the+Alarm) gave Miyajima some offense, even if only one could get past the [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite), but a [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) off the top gave Kadooka a strong took to keep down Miyajima's token horde.

Still, [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) kept him ahead of the pinger, trying to outpace it with more tokens than it could handle. Little did he know, however, that Kadooka had the combo – Kiki Jikki, Mirror Breaker plus either [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) or [Village Bell-Ringer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Village+Bell-Ringer) – in hand.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/88/a7/88a753e16cb5668967849e0501c2287d85494982a1a1816f99dfb6552990b145.jpg)

*Toshiyuki Kadooka has taken Naya Pod this far, could he ride [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) to the finals?* 

Miyajima kept dutifully attacking, but when he tapped out for [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger), all Kadooka had to do was show him the [Village Bell-Ringer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Village+Bell-Ringer) and Kiki Jiki to take game one.

Kadooka 1 – Miyajima 0

### Game 2

[Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) tucked away an early card for Miyajima, and he followed it up with [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue). There were no tokens yet, but they would certainly be forthcoming.

Even if they weren't, it appeared he would have ample time to find them. Kadooka had kept a one land hand after a mulligan without any mana creatures and missed his second and third land drops, all while Miyajima followed up his Virtue with [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession).

When he drew for his next turn, Kadooka missed on land yet again, and rather than prolong the massacre, he opted to try his hand (hopefully a better one) in Game 3.

Kadooka 1 – Miyajima 1

### Game 3

Kadooka quickly mulliganed again, placing his tournament life on the line for a better six cards.

And he could scarcely have asked for a better hand. Turn one Noble Heirarch opened up the way for either a turn two [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) or [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage), both of which were strong openings against WB Tokens. He even had a [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) to give him multiple angles of attack.

![Windbrisk Heights](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Windbrisk+Heights)

And it looked like he would get his way, at least momentarily, as Miyajima simply played [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) and passed.

With several options open to him, Kadooka took the aggressive one, playing out a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) which, thanks to Kadooka's several Exalted creatures, could potentially attack for as much as five the next turn.

[Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution), however, killed [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) and shrunk the [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) temporarily. Kadooka stayed aggressive, casting [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) and the Pridemage, attacking for three with the Finks. He was way ahead on board, but had just the [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) left in hand.

Miyajima's response? Just an [Auriok Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Auriok+Champion). It's Protection from Red was relevant, but it couldn't do much more than watch as [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) marched by and dropped Miyajima to 13 life.

Two Spirits from [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) gained the tokens player two life, but Yasooka's [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) made them look paltry by comparison, at least until it was sent on a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile).

Finally, after holding back the [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) till it found a target, Yasooka released the hasty pinger to dispatch one of the Spirit tokens.

![Cunning Sparkmage](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Cunning+Sparkmage)

The board was no Miyajima's Champion and single token versus [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots), [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) and [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage). It would take a lot of work for the BW player to climb back in the game.

[Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution) was a step in the right direction, offing the Sparkmage, but Kadooka simply cast a second Pridemage and started attacking for chucks of four. But with the Champion repeatedly raising Miyajima's life total, he wasn't getting very far, putting his opponent to 13 once again.

Miyajima used the opportunity to flash back [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), netting two more life and allowing him to attack once again. The little [Auriok Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Auriok+Champion) was keeping him very much in this game.

Another attack from [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) dropped Miyajima to 11, but [Auriok Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Auriok+Champion) kept raising the stakes as [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) entered the battlefield for Yasooka.

That was when Miyajima delivered a haymaker in the form of [Hero of Bladehold](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hero+of+Bladehold). Suddenly, Kadooka was in trouble.

But [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) offered a way out. Probably several ways. That card is really hard to use.

Kadooka chose to use it on [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness), returning the [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling), essentially netting a free 2/1.

![Hero of Bladehold](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Hero+of+Bladehold)

![Chord of Calling](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Chord+of+Calling)

If I could get inside Kadooka's head, I would have bet the plan was to get Kiki-Jiki, copy [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) getting back the Chord again, then finding [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) for the win.

It was a plan, but it would take time, time he might not have if the [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) was something special. He had 13 life, but, as anyone who has played Standard in the past year can attest, [Hero of Bladehold](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hero+of+Bladehold) can make high life totals look silly in a hurry.

Activating [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), Miyajima attacked with everything except the [Auriok Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Auriok+Champion), netting two tokens from the Hero. It was 16 damage represented, though Kadooka had plenty of blockers on the ground.

Kadooka did a lot of blocking, but [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) revealed pretty much his worst-case scenario: [Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution).

In response, he cast [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for Kiki-Jiki and, predictably (hey, I predicted it) copied [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ba/de/bade0cf7eddd0df89eb2345de10d048a819f671a29f83a95733673eb2e4fd773.jpg)

*Jyun'ichi Miyajima was on the cusp of the finals, but had to sweat a tense judge call.* 

Then, a ray of hope. Kadooka drew Kiki-Jiki Mirror breaker off the top! By casting it and copying a [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots), he had enough mana to [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) and the combo. Salvation!

Well, maybe...

After Miyajima had started scooping up his cards, a judge stepped in to point out that one of the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) should have died during the previous attack. The [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) was a 0/5 with a -0/-1 counter on it. The [Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution) made it -1/3, meaning it should have died to an attacking Soldier who was, thanks to the Persecution and Battlecry.

The judges went about consulting with coverage, both Japanese and English, as well as other judges watching the match and, in a bit of fortune, a photographer who happened to capture the exact state of the board during combat.

Even with all of that information, it took a lengthy judge conference and help from the players to reconstruct the game state exactly as it was when the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) should have died. We'll get to the technical explanation in a moment.

Now, with the board set back to the way it should have been, without the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) and the two mana it provides, Kadooka was hard pressed to find the mana necessary to live through the next attack or combo off.

He did the math, fidgeted a bit and, eventually and with a smile, conceded to Miyajima, but moments after he had thought he was on to the finals.

After the match I spoke with both Head Judge Jeff Morrow and Judge Riccardo Tessitori, and both said that, despite the unusual nature of the situation, they were comfortable with the call.

Morrow said the technical ruling was a game rule violation game state backup. He said that, even though the game had progressed, the unusual amount of information at his fingertips let him recreate a game state that otherwise might have advanced too far.

Morrow explained that it was only possible to back up the game to the correct point because of the amount of information he had at his disposal. The photograph of the game state was key, but so was the recollections of coverage and judge staff who were all watching and whose accounts matched up.

Morrow said if it were a match with no one watching and not one closely watched by multiple people with ample evidence to recreate the game state, he would likely not have been able to back it up.

"I felt it was just barely enough information to back it up," he said. "I'm comfortable with the decision."

Tessitori, who was also present in the feature match area, said he concurred with Morrow's decision, but said it was one of the most difficult a judge has to make since two player's tournaments were at stake.

However, both he and Morrow said, it was important that, at this point in the tournament, matches are determined by games of **Magic** and not technicalities or rule mistakes. In the end, the player who would have won the match if the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) had died, as it should have, did win the match.

Miyajima 2 – Kadooka 1

## Finals - Kei Umehara vs. Jyun'ichi Miyajima

*by Chapman Sim* 

On Friday, I secretly told myself that either [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin) or Affinity was going to take down the whole event. How wrong I was. In my defense, probably nobody expected this matchup to make the final tables this weekend, nor had they detected it on their radars.

Kei Umehara's Mono Blue Faeries now faces Jyun'ichi Miyajima's creation, Black White Tokens in the very last round of the largest tournament of Japanese history. It was undeniable that the pair of budding stars were well-prepared to beat metagame.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6d/43/6d4319433239716ddbf28837307f7d05e77a9f267d3403f18b23a020bfa99714.jpg)

*Jyun'ichi Miyajima and Kei Umehara shake hands to get the finals underway.* 

Without the game unraveling, it was hard to decide who had an edge. [Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution), [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) and Lingering Souls are supremely "faerie-unfriendly", as are hand-disruption spells like [Tidehollow Sculler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidehollow+Sculler) and [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize).

However, Faeries (being Faeries) had the potential for the unbeatable nut draw. The full set of [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command)s gave the flying men a chance to race. And with [Engineered Explosives](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Explosives) in Umehara's sideboard, it's hard to predict who will have the last laugh.

### Game One

Miyajima completed the Swiss rounds in the second seed and naturally opts to play first. [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) met with [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand), before being sent to the bottom of the library with [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique). Miyajima shrugged and calmly resolved [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) before laying down [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights), as if mocking the 3/1 faerie. The hidden card was ominous, but there was nothing Umehara could do about it.

When the three spirits attacked, two more ensued, as Miyajima flipped over [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) from under the hideaway land. He declined to flash it back, possibly representing [Zealous Persecution](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Persecution).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/27/9e/279ea2470a0fd006513cb3d5bf5b5c4a480732c60abdf84bfd1080a3e8d5a27a.jpg)

*Kei falls behind in game one.* 

Desperately requiring answers, Umehara flashed in his second [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) targeting himself. Miyajima was undeterred, and simply animated [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and attacked with his four Spirits, reducing his opponent to 12.

Umehara was hanging by a thread and almost out of gas. Holding only double [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) and facing a swarm of tokens, he tried to race by launching a counterattack. Miyajima activated [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) once more and swung with his team ([Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) had stayed back to block). This brought Umehara down to 7 life but in so doing, Miyajima had put both [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) and [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) within [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) range, which Umehara didn't hesitate to use.

In retrospect, it didn't really matter as Miyajima simply flipped over a second [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) and yet another [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), prompting Umehara to pack up his cards.

Kei Umehara 0 – Jyun'ichi Miyajima 1

### Game Two

Keeping a hand containing [Annul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annul) and [Engineered Explosives](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Explosives), Umehara was probably safe for the first few turns, but he needed a fast clock if he wished to achieve victory before things got out of hand.

Miyajima was quick to [Raise the Alarm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raise+the+Alarm), but his next play of [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) was averted by [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite). Afterward, Umehara decided to drop [Engineered Explosives](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Explosives) to safeguard against any future hand-disruption. A second copy of the discard spell resolved, and [Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles) hit the bin.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cf/c7/cfc77327ba34a386fbec225d12b3d7012e5bfd5756f86148e51e1bb876b0e120.jpg)

*A crowd on onlookers examine the game state* 

Umehara needed a creature to defend, and a freshly-drawn [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) would suffice, if only momentarily. Unable to attack with Soldiers anymore, Miyajima took to the skies with two tokens from [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls). This was enough to force his opponent to crack [Engineered Explosives](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Explosives),but he simply flashed [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) back from the yard, before casting a second copy from his hand, replacing all the lost tokens just like that. Miyajima knew about the [Annul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annul), but ran [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue) (from under [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights)) into it anyway, clearing the way for future artifacts or enchantments.

Things took a slight turn when Umehara found a second copy of [Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles). He could effectively steal a token and block with it, killing two tokens with every activation. Thankfully, Miyajima had [Honor of the Pure](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Honor+of+the+Pure) to halve the casualty count, before casting yet another [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/10/6a/106a780f588284cca480de8f0cb80ea6cf5018b4175ccc62bb79d070b7053a54.jpg)

*Jyun'ichi Miyajima bursts through 2 rounds to become the GP Yokohama Champion* 

Umehara knew his dreams of becoming the champion today had been dashed, but he passed the turn anyway, bracing himself for the impending attack. [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) hopped out to chump but Miyajima wasn't going to allow that. [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) sent it away and Umehara wasted no further time in offering a gracious handshake.

Kei Umehara 0 – Jyun'ichi Miyajima 2

Congratulations Jyun'ichi Miyajima. You've just won Grand Prix Yokohama 2012, Japan's largest Grand Prix to date!

## Top 5 Cards

*by Blake Rasmussen* 

![Lingering Souls](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Lingering+Souls)

### 5\. [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls)

Why not? It was good enough to get banned in block constructed, good enough to put multiple people in the Top 8 at Pro Tour *Dark Ascension* and good enough to help Tom Martell hoist a Grand Prix trophy. Why not Modern too? [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) was one of the key additions to Yokohama champion Jyun'ichi Miyajima's Black White Tokens deck, and it was the card that almost single handedly won him his finals match against Kei Umehara. Four 1/1s from one card more often than not turns into four 2/2s or 3/3s, and just as often results in a dead opponent. With every other format besides Vintage mastered, what more could [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) possibly do?

![Cranial Plating](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Cranial+Plating)

### 4\. [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating)

Really, [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) is a stand in for everything in Affinity. [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) is one of the best reasons to play a deck full of artifacts in a format with [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge) and [Hurkyl's Recall](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hurkyl%27s+Recall). The speed the deck offers rivals even [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin)'s best draws, and the all artifact deck proved to be popular on the weekend. The archetype had the best conversion rate from Day 1 to Day 2, giving 18 percent of its players a 7-2 record or better. Modern players would do well to pack more artifact hate from here on out.

![Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker)

### 3\. [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker)

Mr. Mirror Breaker broke out a bit this weekend. Always a card players used as additional, if more expensive, [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin), Kiki-Jiki stepped out of the enchantment's shadow in Yokohama by headlining not only the Blue-Red combo deck, but also the Naya Pod deck that put four players in the Top 8. [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), a far more useful and resilient creature than [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) and [Pestermite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pestermite), put Kiki-Jiki over the top and cemented its position as one of the best creatures in Modern.

![Kitchen Finks](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Kitchen+Finks)

### 2\. [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks)

Best ouphe ever, no contest (take that, [Pyknite](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyknite)!). [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) is an "auto-inclusion" for almost every deck containing [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) or [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains), and for good reason. For just three mana, it typically provides two bodies, while granting the caster four precious life in format that is so damage-oriented. Combined with [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) or [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) or both, the advantage is beyond tremendous. Not to mention the combo with [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) and [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer). Of course, it also mercilessly attacks for three damage, or stays back to defend when necessary, making it one of the most versatile creatures in the format and one of the most commonly played cards this weekend.

![Restoration Angel](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Restoration+Angel)

### 1\. [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel)

Definitely the biggest gem to come out from *Avacyn Restored*, this flashy angel formed the cornerstone of various strategies and found its way into many of the best decks in the format. She's good on her own, but becomes fantastic when used alongside cards which are already great. She was witnessed blinking [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage), [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) and even [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker) all weekend, to devastating effect.

The breadth of decks using her and the surprising uses she found in Modern are what made [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) the top card of the weekend. Naya Pod, UWx [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Snapcaster%2BMage) decks, and virtually any list that could pack white cast the 3/4 at some point this weekend. The top tables were lousy with [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814104924/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), and it might be the card that shifts the Modern metagame for years to come.
